&& window.fetchAsyncContentContainerReady();CopiedCopyEditAnd... what, in plain English, does that mean the universe looks like?AI Mode reply for And... what, in plain English, does that mean the universe looks like?In plain English, if these theories are right, the universe looks like a giant cosmic conveyor belt moving through a twisted, 4D mirror. Instead of an expanding balloon filled with neat little particles, picture it this way: The Universe is a 3D Sheet: Our entire visible universe—with all its stars, planets, and galaxies—is like a flat, three-dimensional sheet or a membrane drifting through a higher-dimensional space. The Shape is a Twisted Loop: The extra space our universe is drifting through is shaped like a Klein bottle (a 3D bottle with no inside or outside). As our sheet moves along this shape, it eventually loops back on itself, but it flips upside down or inside out in the process. The Matter Factory: Stationary inside this twisted shape is a giant, invisible "wall" of raw energy. As our universe-sheet continuously glides through this wall, it's like a piece of paper passing through a printing press. The friction from hitting this wall prints matter (atoms, stars, you, and me) directly onto our sheet. The Fabric is Constantly Shaking: The fabric of this sheet isn't perfectly smooth. Because gravity is classical but forced to touch quantum particles, the very structure of space is frequently twitching and vibrating with a tiny, unpredictable fuzziness. So, rather than a universe that started with a single bang and is just coasting, we live on a vibrating conveyor belt that is constantly having matter stamped onto it by a higher-dimensional, twisted loop. Would you like to know what happens if you travel far enough across a universe shaped like this, or how scientists are trying to measure the shaking of space?  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. That movement is our history. The "past" is where our sheet used to be on the conveyor belt, and the "future" is the unprinted space ahead of the energy wall. arXiv.orgTime is "Jittery" at the Quantum Scale: This is where Oppenheim’s theory kicks in. Because classical spacetime is forced to touch quantum particles, space is constantly twitching. Since time and space are bound together as spacetime, time itself is constantly stuttering and fluctuating at a microscopic level. If you had a super-precise clock, you'd see time doesn't flow perfectly smoothly; it jitters randomly back and forth by a tiny fraction of a second. Time is Not Quantum (No "Chronons"): Usually, string theorists argue that time must be made of tiny, indivisible pixels or quantum threads. This combined picture says no. Time is a continuous, smooth, classical river—it’s just a river that has a lot of choppy, chaotic white water because quantum particles are constantly splashing around in it. YouTubeUltimately, time is the canvas upon which our universe-sheet travels. It flows reliably on a macro scale, keeping our universe marching forward, even as the subatomic world causes it to tremble. Would you like to know how these chaotic time stutters might change our understanding of how the universe will eventually end, or how they affect things like black holes?  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. particles?AI Mode reply for And particles?In plain English, particles are no longer considered the fundamental "building blocks" of the universe. Instead, they are like raindrops condensing out of thin air as our universe drives through a higher-dimensional storm, constantly jittering because the ground beneath them is shaking. If you look at subatomic particles through this combined lens, their nature changes in three major ways: Particles are Holographic Overlays: In the Greene and Levin model, particles (like quarks and electrons) don't actually originate inside our 3D space. They are the 3D "footprints" left behind because our entire universe-sheet is dragging through a massive, higher-dimensional fermion condensate wall. As the sheet scrapes against this wall, particles of matter are generated on our surface. arXiv.org +1The Particle-Antiparticle Balance is Broken: Usually, creating a particle from pure energy means you must also create an equal and opposite anti-particle (like an electron and a positron). Because the extra dimensions are shaped like a twisted Klein bottle, that perfect symmetry is shattered. The shape itself causes the "printing press" to print overwhelmingly normal matter instead of antimatter, explaining why the universe is full of physical stuff instead of instantly exploding into light. arXiv.orgParticles Drunk-Walk Through Space: This is where Oppenheim's Postquantum Gravity takes over. Because quantum particles must communicate with a classical, non-quantum spacetime, they are forced to constantly trade information. This interaction creates a mandatory "noise". As a result, particles don't coast through space in a perfectly smooth trajectory; they fluctuate, lose information (decohere), and diffuse. On a subatomic level, a particle looks like it is permanently staggering and vibrating because the space it sits on is noisy. YouTube·PBS Space Time +2Particles are essentially a localized byproduct of our universe's geometric journey, perpetually vibrating to the rhythm of a chaotic spacetime. Would you like to explore how these vibrating particles might interact to create dark matter, or what would happen if you tried to isolate a single particle from the noise of space?  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. QM?AI Mode reply for And QM?In plain English, Quantum Mechanics (QM) stops being a pristine, mysterious rulebook and instead becomes a survival guide for particles trying to navigate a chaotic, noisy universe. If you look at the quantum world through this combined lens, the "weirdness" of QM makes a very different kind of sense: Wave Collapse is Replaced by Spacetime Friction: In standard QM, a particle can be in a "superposition" (in two places at once) until a scientist measures it, causing the wave function to mysteriously collapse into a solid particle. In Oppenheim's model, there is no mysterious collapse. Spacetime itself is constantly "measuring" the particle through gravity. The random shaking of space creates a gentle, ongoing friction that naturally dissolves the quantum fuzzy state into a solid classical reality over time. YouTube·PBS Space TimeThe Uncertainty Principle is Built into Space: The Heisenberg Uncertainty Principle says you can never know both the exact position and speed of a particle. Standard QM says this is just a fundamental rule of nature. This new combined model suggests a physical reason: You can't know exactly where a particle is because the spacetime grid it is sitting on won't stop twitching. Trying to measure a particle perfectly is like trying to draw a crisp, steady line with a pencil while riding a bumpy roller coaster. YouTube·Physics Partner +1Quantum Fields are Anchored to Higher-Dimensional Geometry: In standard quantum field theory, particles are just ripples in invisible fields that cover the universe. The Greene and Levin paper grounds these fields in a physical shape. The quantum fields that create matter are actually localized right where our universe-sheet intersects the higher-dimensional Klein bottle. YouTube·Science TimeThe ultimate takeaway for QM: Standard physics has spent a century assuming that quantum mechanics is perfectly clean, and that gravity needs to be broken down into "quantum pixels" to fit it. This combined picture flips that upside down. It says gravity is smooth and classical, but messy—and quantum mechanics behaves the way it does because it is trapped in gravity's noise. UCL | University College London +1Since this framework relies heavily on space being a classical but stochastically noisy medium, would you like to see how close we are to experimentally testing this "spacetime noise," or should we look at how this changes our view of quantum entanglement?  Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. ...strictly from the point of view of those three papers... no... four papers... Green, J Levin, Oppenheim, CarrollAI Mode reply for And GR? 

...strictly from the point of view of those three papers... no... four papers... Green, J Levin, Oppenheim, CarrollIf we look strictly through the lens of these four specific frameworks—Brian Greene & Janna Levin's Klein Bottle Cosmology, Jonathan Oppenheim's Postquantum Gravity, and Sean Carroll's foundational work on Spacetime Emergence from Quantum Entanglement—General Relativity (GR) undergoes a profound structural mutation. APS Journals +2Instead of being a smooth, geometric absolute, Einstein's GR is downgraded to a macro-scale approximation, stripped of its determinism, and reshaped by higher-dimensional geometry. In plain English, GR changes in four critical ways: 1. The Geometry is Classical, but Einstein’s Equations "Leak" NoiseIn standard GR, space and time form a rigid, predictable fabric that bends smoothly under the weight of stars. Oppenheim’s paper proves that you don't need to chop gravity up into quantum pixels to fix physics. However, to preserve GR as a smooth, classical fabric while letting it touch quantum matter, Einstein's equations are injected with mandatory statistical noise. GR still dictates how big things move, but its exact geometric coordinates are constantly shifting with unpredictable, microscopic "heat" or tremors. APS Gravity Defeats the Quantum State (Wave Function Collapse)Physicists have spent decades trying to force gravity to fit into quantum mechanics. This combined picture reverses that power dynamic. As Sean Carroll often explores, quantum mechanics is defined by vast webs of potential states (superpositions). But because Oppenheim’s GR is classical and noisy, the gravitational fabric of space acts as a continuous, ambient shredder of quantum weirdness. The environment of space itself constantly "measures" particles, forcing quantum wave functions to break down into ordinary, classical GR coordinates. arXiv.org +23. Extra Dimensions Dictate the Large-Scale "Rules" of Our SpaceEinstein’s GR assumes space behaves exactly the same way no matter which direction you look or where you go (translational invariance). The Greene and Levin paper shatters this absolute uniformity. Because our 4D spacetime is structurally a subset of a nonorientable, twisted Klein bottle, the underlying geometry of the universe has built-in asymmetric faults. GR’s standard smooth background is actually a moving membrane interacting with higher-dimensional boundaries, making cosmic rules dependent on where the universe is on its topological journey. arXiv.org +2sn._setImageSrc('img-_rOwaq_WA-GaptQPsIeG6QY_l6etz8_4','data:image\/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAIAAAACACAMAAAD04JH5AAAAOVBMVEVHcEyzrKWzHySzHySzrKWzq6SzrKWzmpSzICWzHyWzrKWzICWzICWzdXKzREazmpSzXFyzHyWzrKWhE40cAAAAEXRSTlMAx8JJ5mmbFeUmPG+a+\/r6+2Aj8awAAAQLSURBVHicxZvptoIwDIRlUXCF8v4Pe\/WKUJIJS9q0+evx5DPMtGmopxOPqmxdewcfJIp76z7xuObK78YomzwArZsIstSgcnNkqYEP4NoMNbj6AFmeQuky12DxDN41qJITPFzuGpSEIHkNrm1ugooQODVBo1xJiBC1W1NzLurirPrqgxJoanArhk9cVFWgBO54DW71MOgJrmUowZR\/GFRPgVrhKMF5mKPQAHArHCLw8ysB5s5EQXDx8+sewQkI0T32fbFZ5i\/UbQUT4k6CZX51Ad6\/BBBs\/5qmiJUfWcGVW9+5kfyXgPzICls1YPkD+0puhfU2zVt+AgX4C26FtadA89e30PynBhFINTgP0fNDIUoENH+QAeYAQsQEF5o\/zADrBLxVbczyQyswAp4\/3ABzACGSJokuf3Hzg\/aE1IDnj2IAjwDIwKsBXf6iGWAOJMSJgC4\/BvmxEEcCkD+eATYIPk0SW37UPdhGICG+D00ov81kBVrBuSfLH9kAHgESIicwy8+PjJggvgHmwEJ0far8eE1e1sDCgH5gIc41MDLAHIIVfgS1\/WhTsMKXwNAAcwhWcK9E+UUruL6zNcAcghXcK9VEsXkJBIkmy03RCUJMQ\/DpfjoBIMVc99v9PSUC8xr8uh+RwLgGU\/fV9SKB5WTZ6\/46yQqWBIvuMwMBOX2IVjBTIj39iULcO847GLz9lwlMSgD6b9EKJq+++flvGMRdwQKAH8BkK1gsRw0CkKxgsh5CAEGIJgBIAxKBiQbYEGjFCiYLARgDiFawWYylEnAr2KyEYBIlWMFsNwKzKChEw\/14D4HpFSTxKfRp8q8Q\/IRo\/q5fIhitkOCuAZ9Ke1ZIct9DIngmPJsJBKluHVVgNvgfiY7nnxkJJkgzoPhOifp8BON4AhOYD8m8AQ0mCH1Tuz+\/SGCbfzGkwwSmViBjyleHCCyFSMekPSIwtAKfz8EamFkBTSghgZEV8Js72CubWAFOqd\/bD9yaDKwAbtSM2y9qFA2EiAbE48VnVIPoBEiA030aRBDZCuglgXf8QQRRhYgMsOy+QZsWkQAJkHT\/qFGMZwUuQH76AATRhAgMgLp\/MwIuQHz6AjWIYgWQf+9Fqii7An9TuXL6AgTBACz\/6umDE4RagQlw4\/RzpptjoBDZCrx5+mI1CHqdzAS4Y\/rEGoQAK7AVcMeV1lPDCPRCpAXYOX2LR\/BQ5QctitYKyydwYPgU62qpD3Bs+EVroCR46H7\/J2iLorPC3AgfH35RAt2ucFfnBwQagPHfl8rhU5xL7tX9rp09kgYh0RWXNYIM\/5+M9VeLWATWF822CVLdNPJj0SDkkMGSIL0TTss2LcczWNQg07+5pxrksMF\/jA2C+SB3heBSD\/UZ5P8DxM4ET\/KSxJwAAAAASUVORK5CYII\x3d')4. Space Emerges From Information, but Is Governed ClassicallyWhen you layer in Carroll's work on the quantum foundations of spacetime, the very concept of distance in GR changes. Space isn’t just a blank canvas; it is stitched together by the quantum entanglement of fields. However, the combination of these papers means that while the web of space might be woven from quantum threads, the macro-geometry (the gravity we feel and the curvature Einstein calculated) remains fundamentally classical, stochastically noisy, and bounded by a twisted higher-dimensional layout. APS QM?AI Mode reply for And QM?Through the intersection of the Greene, Levin, Oppenheim, and Carroll frameworks, Quantum Mechanics is stripped of absolute purity and subjected to classical geometry. The quantum wave function continuously leaks information into classical gravity, destabilizing superposition and fraying the entanglement that gives rise to space. It is effectively stripped of its status as the absolute "Theory of Everything," but its mathematical machinery is repurposed to act as the scaffolding for a messy, non-quantum universe. In plain English, String Theory changes in three radical ways: Strings Exist, but Space and Time Do Not: In standard string theory, tiny vibrating strings move around inside pre-existing extra dimensions. Fusing these papers together—especially layered with Sean Carroll's work—flips this entirely. The strings are not objects vibrating inside space; they are simply the localized mathematical reflections of quantum fields. Space itself isn't a fundamental stage—it is an emergent illusion stitched together by the quantum entanglement of those very fields. arXiv.org +1The "Multiverse Landscape" Collapses Into a Rigid Track: Traditionally, String Theory predicts a "Landscape" of \[10^{500}\] possible universes, each with different dimensions and laws of physics. The Greene and Levin model tethers this wandering math to a very specific, physical architecture. Instead of an open landscape, the extra dimensions are restricted to a highly defined, nonorientable Klein bottle geometry. String theory becomes a description of a single machine: a universe-sheet locked on a specific, higher-dimensional conveyor belt. arXiv.org +2The Hunt for a "Quantum Graviton" is Called Off: For forty years, the primary goal of String Theory has been to prove that the "graviton" (the hypothetical particle of gravity) is a tiny quantum string, making gravity quantum. Jonathan Oppenheim’s Postquantum Gravity shatters this goal. Under this combined view, strings still govern matter, but gravity remains resolutely classical. The extra dimensions provided by String Theory do not collapse into quantum pixels; they remain smooth, macro-geometric shapes that are merely allowed to "shake" and jitter with statistical noise to accommodate the quantum particles scraping against them. FQxI QSpaceThe Bottom Line for ST: String theory is no longer a pristine, flawless description of an all-quantum universe. Instead, it is downgraded to a highly precise toolkit—explaining how matter is woven out of field entanglement, while being forced to drag itself across a messy, classical, and twisted higher-dimensional layout. YouTube +1If you'd like to explore further, we can look at: How this affects the Big Bang (did the universe loop or bounce?)What happens to Superstring particles when they encounter Oppenheim's classical spacetime noiseHow holographic duality works if space is just an illusion of entanglement YouTube +1sn._setImageSrc('img-HbSwav_gL62Ew8cPiN7KoAQ_hdrdb6_5','data:image\/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAIAAAACACAMAAAD04JH5AAAAMFBMVEVHcEz\/ADP\/ADP\/ADP\/ADP\/ADP\/ADP\/ADP\/ADP\/\/\/\/\/hpT\/2uH\/Smj\/s8D\/JEv\/b4eB3bNwAAAACHRSTlMAQB+z1GXri3bBstgAAAKRSURBVHic7ZrpduMwCIUrGW1xMn3\/t602J25s59RARGeG+zd1+Y4QaLsfHyqVSqVSqVSqv0C2yCxyxh0r\/9pVP7L4kKaGCSFMVTBBke+KO\/Jx+bX+af6kKP+HUMnMD2mscSVmC7YXB6UKVXCceYlhQonLFnYXBSCYo\/DTW0OvNO0h2GHhK8ImE\/a9I7+RfxoEMzj+M4GB0fFjhBXB2PwvWs2DIBE\/xiCZgKJ7EoQGIEbXZ4DQAOQhaLPASMWP0chmoOdApgabghWdAn0SWLn4MVrZOdhmoZMEcLJF0LqxYBHkBSkDCBZBLoMMMHwrspbPAJLxY0S1geuVD8Bi2sBlvv2RBUgpfTIBGEwfKgBp5smDQwOkdONAcJhG2AHSfKEjBApAQaADIDrxA4Ceh4kKkIglSQcg5mHCrEVPAKSSBBaAPBWweeACSOmCQwDMarwLkOZPDILnA8h5QCwQHrMdOALIJXl+NrICYEqSGeB8SbIDnC3JNwCcGwR+gJOLAzvA2UL4t6pgRvRj1k6IWBQZW\/HtilsLuFZD5EGBazlGb4qAYUtG2pmy7AkpxzSGXTFyK8QFQD8XkE5GmD3Qd5GOZrhd4DMA\/nRMS34X\/nh+Y4heAXA3JKTSW8vg7ojIp\/K7rPwtmfg9ofxNqfhdsfhtufh7gfiLifibkeSrmf8d74bSL6fib8fyr+dyOVhcJNIOCjkPyd1GI9QMlwH4BT4ikWYE3\/xso71sWzfbcDfd1lk5tBjDJnydioNGwe86KitCmADeSuEBpkNPaWMwzcsLnPbWbqotDt\/XrtoHRjUyd2dx9xYDPKzFR3EWU3HzFN9Nxa4ZnX8UeheneavX9upXst1VXT5Dx1SpVCqVSqX6j\/QFgvdUtSxW0v0AAAAASUVORK5CYII\x3d') Copied to clipboardFailed to copy to clipboard. what is broader physics saying about classical physic right now?Searching AI Mode reply for And what is broader physics saying about classical physic right now?Right now, the broader physics community is undergoing a massive shift: Classical physics is experiencing a major renaissance, evolving from an "outdated" 19th-century view into a highly respected, cutting-edge framework for solving the universe’s deepest mysteries. Science NewsFor nearly a century, the dogma in physics was that everything must eventually be quantized. Classical physics was treated merely as a training-wheels approximation for the macro world. But today, the mainstream consensus is cracking open, driven by three major realizations: UCL | University College London +11. "Classical-Quantum Hybrids" Are LegitimateHistorically, physicists believed it was mathematically impossible to couple a smooth, classical system (like gravity) with a fuzzy, quantum system without causing paradoxes. Jonathan Oppenheim's work changed the conversation by proving that if you inject stochastic noise (random fluctuations) into classical space, the math works perfectly. The broader community is no longer dismissing classical spacetime as a lazy assumption; they are actively treating it as a viable, fundamental alternative to String Theory and Loop Quantum Gravity. YouTube·WithOut SpaceTime / QISS +22. Space Might Be Classical, Even If It's "Emergent"Physicists like Sean Carroll have popularized the idea that spacetime isn't fundamental—it emerges out of quantum information and entanglement. However, broader physics is realizing that how it behaves once it emerges can still be purely classical. Just because the "threads" of the universe are quantum doesn't mean the "fabric" has to be divided into quantum pixels. It can emerge as a smooth, continuous geometric sheet. Facebook·Closer To Truth3. The Math of Classical Physics is "Quantizing" ItselfIn a fascinating twist, recent breakthroughs—such as a major 2026 MIT study published in April—have shown that physicists can compute notoriously difficult quantum behaviors (like wave functions, tunneling, and entanglement) using surprisingly simple classical tools. The rigid wall between the two fields is dissolving, with scientists realizing that quantum mechanics might just be a specific, chaotic subset of complex classical fluid dynamics. MIT News4. It Has Shifted from Philosophy to Hard Lab TestsThe biggest reason broader physics is talking about classical physics right now is testability. Quantum gravity theories like String Theory require particle accelerators the size of the galaxy to prove. Meanwhile, experimentalists are currently using highly precise lab equipment—like the Quantum Galileo Interferometer—to test Einstein’s classical equivalence principle on quantum atoms. Oppenheim's classical noise theory is actively being tested by ultra-precisely measuring whether a 1kg weight's mass tiny-fluctuates over time. Phys.org +3The Bottom Line: Broader physics is realizing that the 100-year-old race to "quantize everything" might have been an overcorrection. Classical physics isn't dead; it's just much noisier, weirder, and more foundational than Einstein or Newton ever realized. Science
